Appendix A
Initial Conditions

We start from a continuous mass density field in the early matter-dominated epoch. The central objects describing the state of a system are the mass density field
\rho^{(\mathrm{i})}_{m}(\vec{q})
and the peculiar velocity density field
\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})
. The cosmological principle, homogeneity and isotropy, is responsible for a constant mean background mass density and a vanishing mean peculiar velocity of the system, i. e.

\displaystyle\left\langle\begin{pmatrix}\rho^{(\mathrm{i})}_{m}(\vec{q})\\
\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})\end{pmatrix}\right\rangle=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\rho}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{m}\\
\vec{0}\end{pmatrix}\,.

(183)

We can thus describe the mass density field by its fluctuations around the mean background density and introduce the initial density contrast
\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}\,)
defined as,

\rho^{(\mathrm{i})}_{m}(\vec{q})=\bar{\rho}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{m}(1+\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}\,))\,,\quad\text{with}\,,\quad\left\langle\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}\,)\right\rangle=0\,.

(184)

During that early time period, the formation of structures is very well described by linear Lagrangian perturbation theory.
Using linear evolution and the continuity equation, the velocity and mass density-contrast fields can be related to the same initial velocity potential
\psi(\vec{q}^{(\mathrm{i})})
,

\displaystyle\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})

\displaystyle=-D_{+}^{(\mathrm{i})}\Delta\psi(\vec{q}^{(\mathrm{i})})\,,

(185)

\displaystyle\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})

\displaystyle=\dot{D}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{+}\nabla\psi(\vec{q}^{(\mathrm{i})})=H^{(\mathrm{i})}D^{(\mathrm{i})}_{+}\nabla\psi(\vec{q}^{(\mathrm{i})})\,,

(186)

where we used that
\dot{D}_{+}
can be solved by the ansatz
\dot{D}_{+}\propto a
for the growing mode in the radiation dominated Universe at early enough times.
As predicted by inflationary models and confirmed to high accuracy by observations of the temperature-fluctuations in the CMB, we assume the initial velocity potential
\psi^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})
to be a Gaussian random field. This property is inherited by all its derivatives, and consequently, due to (
185
) and (
186
) also by the initial mass and velocity density, which thus follow a joint multivariate Gaussian distribution. The statistical properties of
(\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}),\,\,\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}))
are thus fully described by the mean value and the covariance matrix.
We are finally left with the multivariate Gaussian distribution for the density contrast and the velocity-density field,

\mathcal{P}(\delta^{(\mathrm{i})},\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{(2\pi)^{4}\det(C)}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\begin{pmatrix}\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}),&\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})\end{pmatrix}\cdot C^{-1}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})\\
\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q})\end{pmatrix}\right)\,,

(187)

where the covariance matrix is the single remaining parameter characterizing the distribution. It reads

C=\begin{pmatrix}C_{\delta\delta}&\vec{C}_{\delta\vec{\Pi}}^{\top}\\
\vec{C}_{\vec{\Pi}\delta}&C_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\left\langle\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{1}\,)\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{2}\,)\right\rangle&\left\langle\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{1}\,)\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{2}\,)\right\rangle^{\top}\\
\left\langle\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{1}\,)\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{2}\,)\right\rangle&\left\langle\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{1}\,)\otimes\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{2}\,)\right\rangle\end{pmatrix}\,.

(188)

Homogeneity and isotropy further imply that all of the correlation functions above are functions of relative distances
|\vec{q}_{1}-\vec{q}_{2}|
only and that
\vec{C}_{\delta\vec{\Pi}}(0)=\vec{0}

[
14
]
. The above correlation functions can be related to the two-point density-fluctuation correlation function,

C_{\delta\delta}(|\vec{q}_{1}-\vec{q}_{2}|)=\left\langle\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{1}\,)\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{q}_{2}\,)\right\rangle\,,

(189)

or its Fourier transform, the initial power spectrum
P^{(\mathrm{i})}_{\delta}(k)
, as usual defined as

\left\langle\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{k}_{1})\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{k}_{2})\right\rangle=(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}\right)P^{(\mathrm{i})}_{\delta}(k_{1})\,.

(190)

The density-momentum and the momentum-momentum correlation matrices are then given by

\displaystyle\vec{C}_{\vec{\Pi}\delta}(|\vec{q}_{1}-\vec{q}_{2}|)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{3}\vec{k}_{1}\,\frac{\vec{k}_{1}}{k_{1}^{2}}\,P^{(\mathrm{i})}_{\delta}(k_{1})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\vec{k}_{1}\cdot(\vec{q}_{1}-\vec{q}_{2})}

(191)

\displaystyle C_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(|\vec{q}_{1}-\vec{q}_{2}|)

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{3}\vec{k}_{1}\,\frac{\vec{k}_{1}\otimes\vec{k}_{1}}{k_{1}^{4}}\,P^{(\mathrm{i})}_{\delta}(k_{1})\,\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\vec{k}_{1}\cdot(\vec{q}_{1}-\vec{q}_{2})}\,.

(192)

In order to obtain an initial distribution for individual particles in phase space from the above continuous density field, we fix the total mass
M
of the mass density field and discretize it into
N
particles on a volume
V
, such that
M=Nm
, with the particle mass
m
. In the end, we will be interested in taking the thermodynamic limit
N\rightarrow\infty,\,\,V\rightarrow\infty
, while keeping the mean particle number density
\bar{\rho}=N/V=\mathrm{const.}
. The relation between the mean particle number density and the mean mass density is again given by
\bar{\rho}_{m}=m\bar{\rho}
.
The process of drawing individual particles from the probability density distribution (
148
) is performed by marginalising the conditional probability
\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{x}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}|\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{j}),\,\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{j}))
of finding particle
j
at phase space position
\mathsf{x}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}=(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j},\vec{\mathsf{u}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})
given a specific density contrast
\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}=\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})
and momentum field value
\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}=\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})
at that position, over the joint probability
\mathcal{P}(\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j},\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j})
,

\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{x}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j})=\int\mathrm{d}\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}^{3}\Pi^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}\,\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{x}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}|\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j},\,\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j})\,\mathcal{P}(\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j},\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j})\,.

(193)

Since the density field is decomposed into individual particles, the probability of finding particle
j
at position
\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}
given the density
\rho^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})=\bar{\rho}\left(1-\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})\right)
at that point is simply

\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{q}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}|\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j})=\frac{\rho^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})}{N}=\frac{\bar{\rho}}{N}\left(1-\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})\right)\,.

(194)

Together with the probability
\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{u}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}|\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j})=\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{\mathsf{u}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}-\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})\right)
for a particle at
\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}
to have velocity
\vec{\mathsf{u}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}
, given the velocity field
\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})
at this point, the joint conditional probability has the form

\mathcal{P}(\mathsf{x}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}|\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j},\,\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j})=\frac{\bar{\rho}}{N}\left(1-\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})\right)\,\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{\mathsf{u}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}-\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})\right)\,.

(195)

Generalising to
N
particles and inserting (
195
) and (
187
) in (
193
) we can perform the integration over
\vec{\Pi}^{(\mathrm{i})}(\vec{\mathsf{q}}^{\,(\mathrm{i})}_{j})
right away,

\begin{split}\varrho_{N}(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{x}}^{(\mathrm{i})},\eta^{(\mathrm{i})})=V^{-N}\!\!\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\,&\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}+\mathrm{i}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{u}}^{(\mathrm{i})}\right)\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{C}}}_{\delta\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{C}}}_{\delta\Pi}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\right)\\
&\times\exp\left(\mathrm{i}\sum_{j}\mathsf{t}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z_{j}}\right)\prod_{j=1}^{N}\int\mathrm{d}z_{j}\,z_{j}\,\exp\left(-\mathrm{i}\sum_{j}\mathsf{t}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z_{j}}\,z_{j}\right)\,,\end{split}

(196)

where we have substituted
z_{j}=1-\delta^{(\mathrm{i})}_{j}
and take a Fourier transform introducing the Fourier conjugates
\vec{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}
and
\vec{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}
to
z
and
\vec{\mathsf{u}}^{(\mathrm{i})}
, respectively.
After performing the integration over
z_{j}
and integrating the resulting expression by parts, we obtain

\begin{split}\varrho_{N}(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{x}}^{(\mathrm{i})},\eta^{(\mathrm{i})})=&V^{-N}\!\!\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\,\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}+\mathrm{i}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{u}}^{(\mathrm{i})}\right)(-2\,\pi\mathrm{i})^{N}\times\\
&\times\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}\prod_{j=1}^{N}\frac{\partial}{\partial\,\mathsf{t}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z_{j}}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{C}}}_{\delta\delta}\cdot\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}-\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{C}}}_{\delta\Pi}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}+\mathrm{i}\sum_{j}\mathsf{t}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z_{j}}\right)\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\mathsf{t}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z_{j}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(197)

Since (
197
) is at most quadratic in
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}
, only first- and second-order derivatives can appear in the above expression.
Using this property and performing the integration over
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\mathsf{t}}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{z}
finally allows us to
write the initial phase-space density in the compact form

\varrho_{N}(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{x}}^{(\mathrm{i})},\eta^{(\mathrm{i})})=V^{-N}\int\mathrm{d}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{q}}^{(\mathrm{i})},\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u})\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{p}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}+\mathrm{i}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{u}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{\cdot}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{u}}^{(\mathrm{i})}\right)\,,

(198)

with the correlation function

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{\mathsf{q}}^{(\mathrm{i})},\boldsymbol{\mathsf{t}}^{(\mathrm{i})}_{p})=

\displaystyle\prod_{j=1}^{N}\left(1-\vec{\mathsf{C}}_{\delta_{j}u_{k}}\cdot t_{u_{k}}\right)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,k=1\\
j\neq k\end{subarray}}^{N}\mathsf{C}_{\delta_{j}\delta_{k}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq j,k\end{subarray}}^{N}\left(1-\vec{\mathsf{C}}_{\delta_{l}u_{s}}\cdot t_{u_{s}}\right)

(199)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,k=1\\
j\neq k\end{subarray}}^{N}\mathsf{C}_{\delta_{j}\delta_{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b=1\\
a\neq b\\
a,b\neq j,k\end{subarray}}^{N}\mathsf{C}_{\delta_{j}\delta_{k}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1\\
l\neq j,k,a,b\end{subarray}}^{N}\left(1-\vec{\mathsf{C}}_{\delta_{l}u_{s}}\cdot t_{u_{s}}\right)+\cdots\,.

The correlation matrices
\mathsf{C}_{\delta_{j}\delta_{k}}\,,\vec{\mathsf{C}}_{\delta_{j}p_{k}}\,,\mathsf{C}_{p_{j}p_{k}}
generalize the entries of the covariance matrix (
149
) to the
6N
-dimensional phase-space by evaluating (
149
) at the respective particle positions,

\begin{split}\mathsf{C}_{\delta_{j}\delta_{k}}=C_{\delta\delta}(|\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{j}-\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{k}|)\,,\,\,\,&\,\,\,\vec{\mathsf{C}}_{\delta_{j}u_{k}}=\vec{C}_{\delta\vec{\Pi}}(|\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{j}-\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{k}|)\,,\\
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}=\mathsf{C}_{u_{j}u_{k}}\otimes(e_{j}\otimes e_{k})\,,\,\,\,&\,\,\,\text{with}\,\,\,\mathsf{C}_{u_{j}u_{k}}=C_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(|\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{j}-\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{k}|)\,.\end{split}

(200)

By going to spherical coordinates, we can write

\begin{split}\vec{\mathsf{C}}_{\delta_{j}u_{k}}&=C_{\delta\vec{\Pi}}(\mathsf{r})\,e_{\mathsf{r}}\,,\\
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}&=C_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(\mathsf{r})\,e_{\mathsf{r}}\otimes e_{\mathsf{r}}=C^{xx}_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(\mathsf{r})\,(\mathbb{1}-e_{\mathsf{r}}\otimes e_{\mathsf{r}})+C^{zz}_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(\mathsf{r})\,e_{\mathsf{r}}\otimes e_{\mathsf{r}}\,,\end{split}

(201)

with the radial unit vector
e_{\mathsf{r}}
and radial distance
\mathsf{r}=|\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{j}-\vec{\mathsf{q}}_{k}|
. The matrix
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}
thus takes on a diagonal form

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}=\begin{pmatrix}C^{xx}_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(\mathsf{r})&0&0\\
0&C^{xx}_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(\mathsf{r})&0\\
0&0&C^{zz}_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(\mathsf{r})\end{pmatrix}\,.

(202)

The auto-correlation entries of
\boldsymbol{\mathsf{C}}_{uu}
are simply given by
C^{xx}_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(0)=C^{zz}_{\vec{\Pi}\otimes\vec{\Pi}}(0)=\sigma^{2}_{u}
with the the velocity dispersion

\sigma^{2}_{u}\coloneq\frac{1}{3}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}k}{(2\,\pi)^{3}}\frac{P^{(\mathrm{i})}_{\delta}(k)}{k^{2}}\;.

(203)

Appendix B
Free Cumulants

Following
[
1
]
, we provide general expressions for the free cumulants for a homogeneous system which are necessary in order to compute the density-fluctuation power spectrum at tree- and one-loop level. By inserting the appropriate trajectories and initial reduced phase-space densities derived in Sec.
V
, the general expressions are be specified to the cosmological system. In (
157
) and (
158
) the tree-level cumulants for cosmology are explicitly given. Higher-order free cumulants for cosmology are then obtained analogously from the general expressions below.

\displaystyle G_{f}^{(0)}(S_{1})=

\displaystyle\,(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}\right)\bar{\rho}\,\varphi(\vec{l}_{1},t^{(\mathrm{i})})\,,

(204)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G_{ff}^{(0)}(S_{1},S_{2})=&\,(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}\right)\left[\bar{\rho}\,\varphi\bigg(\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}\bigg)\right.\\
&\left.+\bar{\rho}^{2}\,\widetilde{g}_{2}\bigg(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m},t^{(\mathrm{i})}\bigg)\right]\,,\end{split}

(205)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{(0)}_{fff}(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})&=(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}\right)\left[\bar{\rho}\,\varphi\bigg(\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m}\bigg)\right.\\
&\left.+\bar{\rho}^{2}\,\varphi\bigg(\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}\bigg)\widetilde{g}_{2}\bigg(\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m},\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m},t^{(\mathrm{i})}\bigg)\right.\\
&\left.+\bar{\rho}^{2}\,\varphi\bigg(\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}\bigg)\widetilde{g}_{2}\bigg(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m},t^{(\mathrm{i})}\bigg)\right.\\
&\left.+\bar{\rho}^{2}\,\varphi\bigg(\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m}\bigg)\widetilde{g}_{2}\bigg(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m},t^{(\mathrm{i})}\bigg)\right.\\
&\left.+\bar{\rho}^{2}\,\widetilde{g}_{3}\bigg(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m},\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m}t^{(\mathrm{i})}\bigg)\right]\,,\end{split}

(206)

\displaystyle G_{f\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}(S_{1},S_{2})=

\displaystyle\,(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}\right)(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{2}\right)b(1,2)\,G_{f}^{(0)}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m})\,,

(207)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{(0)}_{f\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}}(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})=&\,(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}\right)(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{2}\right)\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{3}\right)\bigg(b(1,2)+b(3,2)\bigg)\times\\
&\times\bigg(b(1,3)+b(2,3)\bigg)G_{f}^{(0)}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m})\,,\end{split}

(208)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{(0)}_{f\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}}(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3},S_{4})&=(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4}\right)(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{2}\right)\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{3}\right)\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{4}\right)\times\\
&\times\bigg(b(1,2)+b(3,2)+b(4,2)\bigg)\bigg(b(1,3)+b(2,3)+b(4,3)\bigg)\bigg(b(1,4)+b(2,4)+b(3,4)\bigg)\times\\
&\times\,G_{f}^{(0)}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m})\,,\end{split}

(209)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{(0)}_{ff\mathcal{B}}(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3})&=\,(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}\right)(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{3}\right)\times\\
&\times\left[b(1,3)\,G^{(0)}_{ff}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m})\right.\\
&\left.+b(2,3)\,G^{(0)}_{ff}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m})\right]\,,\end{split}

(210)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{(0)}_{ff\mathcal{B}\mathcal{B}}(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3},S_{4})&=(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4}\right)(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{3}\right)\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{4}\right)\left[\bigg(b(1,3)b(1,4)+b(1,3)b(3,4)\right.\\
&\left.+b(4,3)b(1,4)\bigg)G^{(0)}_{ff}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m})\right.\\
&\left.+\bigg(b(2,3)b(2,4)+b(2,3)b(3,4)+b(4,3)b(2,4)\bigg)\times\right.\\
&\left.\times G^{(0)}_{ff}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m})\right.\\
&\left.+b(1,3)b(2,4)\,G^{(0)}_{ff}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m},\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m})\right.\\
&\left.+b(2,3)b(1,4)\,G^{(0)}_{ff}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m},\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m})\right]\,,\end{split}

(211)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G^{(0)}_{fff\mathcal{B}}(S_{1},S_{2},S_{3},S_{4})&=(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4}\right)(2\pi)^{3}\delta_{\mathrm{D}}\left(\vec{l}_{4}\right)\times\\
&\times\left[b(1,4)\,G^{(0)}_{fff}(\vec{k}_{1}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m},\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m})\right.\\
&\left.+b(2,4)\,G^{(0)}_{fff}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{2}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m},\vec{k}_{3},\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m})\right.\\
&\left.+b(3,4)\,G^{(0)}_{fff}(\vec{k}_{1},\vec{l}_{1}+\vec{k}_{1}\frac{T_{1}}{m},\vec{k}_{2},\vec{l}_{2}+\vec{k}_{2}\frac{T_{2}}{m},\vec{k}_{3}+\vec{k}_{4},\vec{l}_{3}+\vec{k}_{3}\frac{T_{3}}{m}+\vec{k}_{4}\frac{T_{4}}{m})\right]\,.\end{split}
